LEAD TAKES the full version: tilt span 0.50 → **0.62** & `cast1`/`rebound1` maxDistance 3.4 → **3.9** together (28% tighter, height-free ratio), ⊥ the minimal 0.56 (15% tighter, HALF THE RING GOES STEEL @ peak — a visible defect the owner would report). **(4) TRAILS: threshold 0.16, softness 0.10 ∴ FOOT 0.11, & the pool-lit + moonlit terrain tops out @ linear luma 0.102** ∴ the soft edge starts ABOVE the ground's maximum & the ground contributes EXACTLY NOTHING while everything the eye reads as glow trails. loop BOUNDED: gain +0.90 ∴ steady state = injection/(1−0.90); `smear1` alpha [0,0.47] @ f60 & [0,0.50] @ f800 — FLAT, ⊥ a short window (§V631); sink alpha [0.785,1.000] INSIDE [0,1]. & trails do better than predicted: **amber hard-flip 2.9% → 0.5% (f400), 4.5% → 1.0% (f1000), total energy −1.6% (DOWN, ⊥ up)**. rim: 0 void px ∈ the lower 45% @ all 8 angles (T658 holds). node delta **+7**|V668
T712|x|**node BODY carries a VERY SUBTLE type-family TINT ∴ 3D | point | texture | value read apart @ a glance.** owner: "nodes of different types could use a very very subtle hint of different background color so we can easily distinguish 3d/point/texture nodes etc". **TECHNIQUE: shift HUE @ near-CONSTANT LUMINANCE** — a brightness difference would make some families read as MORE IMPORTANT ∨ closer, which is a lie; equal-L hue is the only honest carrier for a peer distinction. **BOUND IT BOTH WAYS & GATE BOTH**: a MINIMUM Δ between families (∨ it repeats §T664 — made quiet, became INVISIBLE) & a MAXIMUM Δ from the base surface (§V90: the owner said "very very subtle" TWICE). **⊥ FIGHT the existing colour carriers** — the component top-edge (T639/`--component`), the status dot, selection ring & error state ALL already speak colour ∴ the body tint sits UNDER them & ! collide. & the tint is REDUNDANT reinforcement, ⊥ the sole carrier: the node already prints its TYPE ∴ colour-blind readers lose nothing. families ← the registry's own port/payload kinds, ⊥ a hand list (§V487 & T675's `satisfies` precedent: a NEW kind ! be a TYPECHECK failure until it declares a tint). both themes, §V17 tokens only|T708,V90,V17,T639,T675 ⟶ **DONE `7474a9d`.** hue @ CONSTANT luminance: **max lightness drift 0.14 L\***, min pair **6.67**, max from surface **6.37** ∴ bounded BOTH ways as specified. 5 directions around `--bg-raise` ∈ the a/b plane, **each pointed @ the hue its PORT FAMILY ALREADY USES** ∴ the tint REINFORCES §V26 instead of adding a 2nd code. **KINDS ARE GROUPED, ⊥ tinted 1-for-1** — 15 hues @ one luminance inside a subtle radius separate by UNDER A UNIT each & VANISH (T664 again). & **it LOOKED @ it**: side by side the 6 are clearly distinct, individually none shouts & none reads brighter than another|V705,T664,V26
T713|x|**DEPTH OF FIELD for E34-Lidar — node ∨ shader. owner: "a depth of field effect either via node or shader could be well interesting to add here for the lidar scene".** **⚠ THE KEY CALL, & it is ⊥ the obvious one: for a POINTS/BEAMS scene the right DoF is SCATTER, ⊥ a post-process GATHER.** a gaussian blur turns bright out-of-focus points into DIM SMEARS; what the eye wants — & what sells a lidar's glowing returns — is **BOKEH: a bright out-of-focus point becomes a DISC**, which a gaussian CANNOT produce. & points are ALREADY SPRITES ∴ **grow `sizePixels` w/ the circle of confusion `|depth − focus|` & DIM by area to conserve energy** ∴ true bokeh, ⊥ depth texture, ⊥ gather pass, ⊥ new node — it is arithmetic ∈ the kernel that already sizes the sprite. ⚠ interacts w/ §B132 (points `scale` was inert until today) & T680's `taper`. **the TERRAIN is a SURFACE ∴ it would still need the post version** — decide whether it defocuses at all: for a lidar the RETURNS are the subject & a sharp ground reads fine, so scatter-only may be the whole answer & the cheaper one. IF a post node is wanted anyway: it needs DEPTH as a texture ∴ FIRST establish whether the scene synthesis (`synthesis.depth`) EXPOSES one — that is the node-level blocker & a lead decision, ⊥ an improvisation|T711,B132,T680,V644 ⟶ ⚠**LEAD PREMISE BROKEN, measured from the COMPILED PLAN ⊥ read: E34's glowing returns are ⊥ SPRITES ∴ "points are already sprites, grow `sizePixels` w/ the CoC" does ⊥ apply here.** `impacts1`/`echoes1` are INSTANCES & `rays1`/`bounce1` BEAMS; size is `instance.x`, a **PER-DRAW UNIFORM**, & `geometry.scale` is `type:"number"` w/ **⊥ map binding**, unlike `tint`. there is **⊥ per-instance size channel anywhere ∈ the scene path** (the only per-instance buffers are positions, endpoints, pointColors & group attrs). & E34's only `renderPoints` (`poolmap1`) splats into a 512² ALBEDO texture, never the picture — & could ⊥ be the answer anyway ∵ §T642: a renderPoints overlay composited over a 3D camera MISALIGNS. **∴ SCATTER DoF COSTS EXACTLY ONE NODE CHANGE — a MAPPABLE `scale` on `geometry`, the precise mirror of `tint`'s existing `{mode:"map", bindings:{static, map:{attribute}}}` slot** (already binds as `pointColors`): generalises to instances, points AND beams, 1 more buffer on a draw already carrying 3, & the CoC then lives ∈ the kernel that already places the mark. **LEAD APPROVES.** & §B132 honoured by MEASUREMENT ∈ the pair shape it demands: 4 authored scales (.052/.030/.013/.006) appear as 4 distinct `instance.x`, taper 0 for primaries & 1 for bounce legs|T721,B132,T642,T680 ⟶ **BUILT & MEASURED; LEAD ACCEPTS (c): DO ⊥ PUT DoF INTO E34 @ A VISIBLE STRENGTH.** the MECHANISM works; **the SUBJECT is the problem & it is STRUCTURAL** — E34's subject is a RING spanning ~2–6 units from a camera @ 4 ∴ a depth of field shallow enough to SEE defocuses MOST OF THE SUBJECT. **bokeh sells when a FEW lights are out of focus AGAINST SOMETHING SHARP; here the out-of-focus set IS THE PICTURE.** & **DIMMING BY AREA is what makes it BOKEH rather than a bigger dot — & is exactly what drops the marks UNDER `cut1`'s 0.42 bloom knee that the last 2 rounds spent getting them ABOVE**: moderate −42% of the above-knee population & peak 0.624→0.500 (flat cutouts, glow GONE); size-only w/o dimming +105% & peak held = bigger BRIGHTER dots, ⊥ bokeh. ⚠**& an UNPREDICTED COUPLING: echoes-only DIMS THE BOUNCE BEAMS** ∵ they read `mark2a`'s tint unchanged — **that IS T711's colour coherence, approved this morning** — ∴ any per-point brightness change to an echo reaches the beam ENDING on it & they render as DARK GREEN LINES; fixing it needs a 5th attribute on `mark2a`, **refused @ 4 (§V588)** ∴ **the coherence & the energy conservation are ∈ DIRECT CONFLICT BY CONSTRUCTION**. ∴ T721 stands on its OWN & wants a home where the subject sits @ ONE depth against a background — §T727's E37 is its 1st consumer|T721,V588,V689
T714|x|⚠**TOP — the whole system GLITCHES/CHOKES/STUTTERS/LAGS while adjusting ANY parameter on ANY node.** owner: "is that something we can improve or just in the nature of how things are here?" **ANSWER: ⊥ INHERENT. a parameter change ! be a UNIFORM WRITE — ⊥ recompile, ⊥ plan rebuild (§V5). TD does this @ 60fps on far bigger graphs.** **LEADS, ⊥ conclusions**: (a) `use-graph-compile.ts:33` states the design assumption OUT LOUD — *"The compiler is pure and cheap, so it runs on every revision"* ∴ **that assumption is the suspect @ 70 nodes** (E24) & a drag emitting a revision per pointermove. (b) a VALUES-ONLY path ALREADY EXISTS (`classifyGraphChange`/`isValuesOnly`, & "the same plan structurally; only its pass uniform VALUES") ∴ **FIRST QUESTION: is it actually TAKEN during a drag, ∨ does everything classify STRUCTURAL?** (c) **§T519's precedent is the shape to check first**: `graphEdits` compared by OBJECT REFERENCE ∴ a JSON parse ALWAYS yielded `topology` — a reference-comparison fault would make EVERY drag structural while the classifier LOOKS correct. CPU profile of the drag (n=44,501 samples / 5,917 ms): **React render+commit 56.6% = 3,346 ms** vs **`compileGraph` 12.7% = 749 ms**; idle control React 5.1% = 311 ms ∴ **the drag delta is React 311 → 3,346 ms (10.8×)** against compileGraph 452 → 749. counts: **174 context identity changes, 29,118 `NodeView` renders (146/frame on 70 nodes), 46,267 `getBoundingClientRect` (230/frame), 35,757 DOM mutations**. **MECHANISM: `NodeView` is `memo`'d, its payload is deliberately `{nodeId}`, & `derive.ts` returns the PREVIOUS projection when nothing changed — every word true & NONE of it worked, ∵ `NodeView` also calls `useGraphCanvas()` & a CONTEXT VALUE RE-RENDERS EVERY CONSUMER STRAIGHT THROUGH `memo`.** `renderPreview` closed over `graph`; `previewInspect` over 2 `Set`s rebuilt from each compile ∴ the context value was NEW on every revision. **FIX** (`graph-pane.tsx` only, 57+/17−): `renderPreview` reads `graphRef.current`; the 2 sets keep their object when MEMBERSHIP does ⊥ move. **RESULT: frame p50 19.3 → 11.8 ms, p95 23.5 → 18.1; the drag's ADDED per-frame cost 11.0 → 3.5 ms = 68% removed; context changes 174 → 0; NodeView renders/frame 146 → 21; gBCR 230 → 37; LONG TASKS 20 (mean 80 ms) → 0.** lead-by-lead: (a) compiler wrong suspect; (b) values-only path IS taken (`uniform-update`, `isValuesOnly`, backend compile skipped — §V5 holds); (c) right SHAPE wrong FILE; (d) value graph 0.097 ms/frame = 0.6%; (e) coalescing WORKS (174 revisions / ~200 frames); (f) §T313 still worth doing & ⊥ this row's fix (~1.6 ms of the remaining 3.5)|V673,V674

ADAPT TO ZOOM ∨ it is useless zoomed out.** owner: "we probably should adapt the grid size of the background of the chart to the zoom level so it doesnt become useless at further zoom outs? if thats possible without a lot of hassle". **YES & it IS cheap** — we already use React Flow's `<Background>` (T463 puts it ∈ the negative-z slot) ∴ read `useViewport().zoom` & derive `gap`. **the standard form: OCTAVE LOD — `gap = base · 2^ceil(−log2(zoom))`** ∴ screen-space spacing stays ~constant @ any zoom. ⚠**GOTCHA: a QUANTISED gap POPS @ each threshold** — the dots jump size/spacing @ a zoom boundary & that reads as a GLITCH, which is worse than a useless grid. ∴ **2 STACKED `<Background>` layers @ gap & 2×gap w/ the FINE one's opacity CROSS-FADING out as zoom falls** — React Flow stacks them by `id`, so it is 2 components & 1 derived opacity, ⊥ custom drawing. keep §T463's properties: above the dots' own ground, BENEATH every node & edge, `pointer-events: none`, & FULL brightness on the owner's call. pairs w/ T708's dot-contrast work — SAME worker, ∵ a grid that adapts & is invisible is still useless|T463,T708,V90 ⟶ **DONE `d3ba0d2`, & THE SECOND HALF WAS FOUND BY SCREENSHOT, ⊥ ARITHMETIC: React Flow scales `size` BY ZOOM TOO** ∴ spacing was correct while each dot was **0.24px @ zoom 0.16** — still a flat ground, EVERY NUMBER LOOKING RIGHT. §V655's shape again ∴ divide the dot size by zoom as well. measured live 1 → 0.118: coarse screen pitch stays ∈ [16,32)px where a FIXED gap reached **1.9px**. §V668 checked & does ⊥ bite: both grid SVGs measured `rgba(0,0,0,0)` ∴ the fade acts on DOTS only, never an opaque ground
T718|x|**E13-Prism round 2 — owner: "prism is missing the whole glass effect where we see whats happening inside the prisma and the illumination of the environment and background. its getting there but still pretty weak. improve this."** 2 named gaps. **(a) NOTHING IS DRAWN INSIDE.** T710 states it: **the beams are drawn 0.05 IN FRONT of the front face** ∴ the interior is empty by construction & the glass has ⊥ INTERIOR TO SEE INTO. ∴ draw the INTERNAL SEGMENT — entry point → exit face — WITHIN the volume, dimmer than the outside shaft. it reads as light-inside-glass ∵ the body is near-black & edge-rimmed, so a bright thread inside a dark rimmed form IS the glass cue; ⊥ transparency needed. + the 3 places a real prism shows its work: an ENTRY FLARE where the white shaft strikes the face, an EXIT-FACE GLOW ∈ spectrum colours, & TIR GLINTS along the base. **(b) THE SPECTRUM LIGHTS NOTHING.** ∴ ATMOSPHERE: a WIDE, VERY DIM, BLURRED copy of the fan BEHIND it (scattering — the reference's beams glow into the air around them), & SPILL where the fan passes the ground. ⚠ cheap ∵ the bands are ALREADY beams — a 2nd draw @ larger taper & low intensity, ⊥ a new mechanism. ⚠ §V668: a beam is OPAQUE ∴ a dim WIDE beam paints DARK over anything behind it — the atmosphere copy !
